# App library reference (continent-app/src/lib)

Generated by `python -I scripts/docs/build_reference.py` from the source (JSDoc and leading comments, read as text). Do not edit by hand (ROADMAP B6, T1167). The rules and thresholds behind these functions are in `docs/HANDBOOK.md` and `docs/logic/`.

291 modules, 2117 exports; 1653 have a comment above them.

## `src/lib/steps.js`

steps.js, walking distance in the unit travellers actually feel. Kilometres are a map unit. Nobody knows whether 9 km is a hard day, but everybody with a phone in their pocket knows what 15,000 steps costs them: the step count is the one walking figure most people already have a calibrated sense of,

| Kind | Name | Summary |
|---|---|---|
| const | `STEPS_PER_KM` | An adult's walking stride is roughly 0.70 to 0.78 m, which puts a kilometre between about 1,300 and 1,400 steps. 1,350 is the middle of that band and keeps the round numbers round: 5 km reads as 6,750, and the offered budgets (5k, 10k, 15k, |
| function | `kmToSteps` | Kilometres to steps, rounded to the nearest 500 so nothing reads false-precise. |
| function | `stepsToKm` | Steps back to kilometres, one decimal, for the server contract. |
| function | `formatSteps` | "9,500 steps" with the reader's own thousands separator. The approximate sign is the caller's business: some strings already say "about". |
| function | `kmToStepsLabel` | Kilometres straight to a formatted step count, the common path. |
| const | `SHORT_WALK_KM` | Under this, a walk is better described by how long it takes than how far it goes: "12 min walk" beats "0.9 km". |
| const | `WALK_KMH` | 4.5 km/h is the usual town-walking figure, and matches daySchedule.js. |
| function | `walkMinutes` | Minutes on foot for a short hop, at least 1. |
| function | `distanceAway` | How far away something is, said the way it is actually experienced. Under a kilometre and a half nobody thinks in distance, they think in minutes: "an 11 minute walk" is a decision, "0.8 km" is a sum. Beyond that the distance is the better |

Three destinations, because walkers do not all use the same thing: GPX 1.1 the interchange format every hiking app reads (Komoot, Gaia GPS, Garmin Connect, OsmAnd, Organic Maps, Suunto). Carries the 

| Kind | Name | Summary |
|---|---|---|
| function | `trailFileBase` | "theth-qerec-curraj-i-eperm-63478", safe on every filesystem. |
| function | `trailGpx` | The trail as GPX 1.1: one <trk> with a <trkseg> per line segment, elevation written wherever the wire measured it, the city-day stops as <wpt>s in visiting order, and the route's own attribution in <copyright>. detail is the full-resolution |
| function | `splitTourByStages` | A tour's line cut into one point list per stage. Each stage carries start_m and end_m along the tour, measured on the full line the pipeline composed; the line on the wire is a simplification, so its own length is scaled onto the stages' to |
| function | `cycleGpx` | A cycle route or tour as GPX 1.1. `tracks` is a list of { name, lines } where lines are coordinate lists; a route passes one track, a tour one per day. The credit travels inside the file (ODbL, and the EuroVelo credit where it applies), the |
| function | `cycleFileBase` | A file name for a cycle route or tour, the trail rule applied. |
| function | `lineParts` | The line parts of a cycle geometry, for callers that write a route GPX. |
| function | `trailFactLine` | The one line of facts the KML carries: "12.1 km, 7 h, +1568 m". The climb is read uphill (trailClimb().up), the same number the trail card prints, so Mount Korab (9), drawn summit to village, says +1423 m rather than +7 m (T108-d). Composed |
| function | `trailKml` | The trail as KML: the line, a start and finish pin, and the stop list in the line's description panel. One folder, so My Maps imports it as one layer the traveller can rename. |
| function | `downloadTextFile` | Download text as a file. Kept here rather than in the caller so the object URL is always revoked, even when the click is cancelled. |
| function | `shareOrDownloadFile` | Hand a file to the phone's own share sheet when it can take files (that is how a GPX reaches a hiking app on iOS and Android), and fall back to a plain download everywhere else. Resolves 'shared' or 'downloaded'. |
| function | `trailShareUrl` | The link that reopens this trail. The hash (not the query) so the payload never reaches a server log and never collides with the browse-state params useUrlSync writes. |
| function | `trailheadDirectionsUrl` | Google Maps directions to the trailhead, by coordinates: a "<trail name>, <country>" search lands on "can't find this place" for most of these. |
| function | `shareTrailLink` | Share the link through the phone's share sheet, or copy it. Resolves 'shared', 'copied', or null when neither is available. |
| function | `stopNamesOf` | Stop names for a city day, in visiting order, for the export files. |

## `src/lib/trailGeo.js`

trailGeo.js, the geometry a drawn route needs at runtime. The wire ships a line and its totals; following that line in the app asks questions the wire cannot answer: how far along a GPS fix is, how much climbing is left, whether the walker has wandered off the path. Everything here measures against 

| Kind | Name | Summary |
|---|---|---|
| function | `metresBetween` | Great-circle metres between two [lon, lat] pairs. |
| function | `routePoints` | One flat point sequence for a LineString or MultiLineString, each point carrying the distance walked to reach it: [{ lon, lat, ele, m }] ele is null when the wire ships 2D coordinates Points that repeat the previous position are dropped (th |
| const | `routeLength` | Metres walked over the whole sequence. |
| function | `nearestOnRoute` | Project a fix onto the route: how far along it sits (m), how far off the line it is (offM), and the point on the line itself. Segments that only bridge two MultiLineString pieces carry no walked distance and are skipped, so a fix never land |
| function | `basesAlong` | Our own destinations along a route: the "Bases along the route" list (ROUTES.md R7). Ordered by distance ALONG the line rather than by distance from it, so the list reads as an itinerary: the towns in the order you would meet them, each wit |
| function | `sliceRoute` | The line split at a distance along it: what has been walked and what is left, both as [lon, lat] arrays ready for a GeoJSON source. The split point is added to both halves so the two lines meet. |
| function | `remainingRelief` | The climbing and the dropping still ahead after a point on the route, from the wire's profile ([[distM, eleM], ...]). Both, because the time estimate below charges for descent too. Null without a profile. |
| function | `hikeTimeMin` | DIN 33466, the walking-time rule the wire itself used: 4 km/h flat, 300 m/h up, 500 m/h down, then the larger of the two times plus half the smaller. Reproducing it here means a remaining-time estimate on the trail agrees with the total pri |
| function | `isLoopRoute` | True when the route ends where it started, allowing for a car park's worth of slack on a short walk and a little more on a long one. |

Trip length is not a filter that drops trips: every trip exists at two lengths (lib/tripLength.js, T101), so a length cannot exclude one. It is the length the list is read at. ## `src/lib/when.js`

T649: the traveller's "when", read in ONE place. The settings line holds three kinds of answer (exact dates, a month, any time) and the Explore month filter (xm) can set a month of its own. Every surface that speaks of "the month" (the Crowds stat, the Explore quiet flag, the month fact, the rails) 

| Kind | Name | Summary |
|---|---|---|
| function | `resolveWhen` | @param {object} s { dateFlex, departDate, returnDate, ownDates, xm } xm is the Explore month filter (1 to 12); when set it wins. |
| function | `xmFromSearch` | The Explore month filter (xm) from a query string ("?tab=map&xm=11"), or null. The destination page reads it here so it follows the filter the reader came from (ExploreTab keeps xm in the URL). |
| const | `whenMonth` | The single month the traveller has (the start month), or null. This is the value App.jsx publishes as travelMonth. |
| function | `busiestMonth` | The month a per-month series should be read at: the one month, or for a trip spanning two the busier of them by `share` (crowd share), else the first. Null for 'any'. |
